A dependency-free helper used to build the HTTP Basic auth header for Zulip; it exists so notifications need no extra package. Not intended as a general-purpose codec.

Mera.simulationrecord — Method
simulationrecord(info::InfoType) -> SimulationRecord
simulationrecord(path::String; outputs=nothing, verbose=true) -> SimulationRecord

A machine-readable description of a simulation: which code, box and resolution, units, cosmology, which physics was switched on, which data the outputs hold, and the SHA-256 of the run's input files. Where a sidecar describes one result, this describes the simulation behind it.

Arguments

  • info::InfoType: one output, from getinfo. The record describes that output.
  • path::String: a simulation folder. The record of its last output, plus a run part that lists every output with its time, redshift and size on disk, and the outputs at which the namelist changed (a restart with new settings).

Keywords (folder form)

  • outputs = nothing: the output numbers to include; nothing takes every output in the folder.
  • verbose::Bool = true: say how many outputs were read.

Returns

A SimulationRecord. Index it like a dictionary, e.g. rec["physics"]["summary"].

The physics part comes from the code's reader. For RAMSES it is read from the namelist and, when makefile.txt holds them, the makefile variables; the namelist values are cleaned (comments removed, .true. and numbers parsed). Its summary uses the same keys for every code: hydro, mhd, gravity, particles, radiative_transfer, cooling, star_formation, feedback, sinks; each is false, true, the settings of that process, or nothing when unknown.

Examples

rec = simulationrecord(getinfo(300, "/data/sim"))      # one output
rec["resolution"]["finest_cell_pc"]
rec["physics"]["summary"]["star_formation"]

run = simulationrecord("/data/sim")                    # the whole run
run["run"]["outputs"]                                  # number of outputs
datacite(run; creators=["Behrendt, Manuel"], title="Isolated disc galaxy", file="datacite.json")

See also datacite, writesimulationrecord, provenance.

Mera.slice — Function
slice(obj, var, [unit]; ...) -> CoveringGridResult  (axis-aligned)  |  NamedTuple  (off-axis)

A single, non-integrated cutting plane through AMR cell data (HydroDataType, GravDataType, RtDataType). One name, two modes, chosen automatically from the keywords:

Axis-aligned (default). slice_axis=:z, slice_pos=0.5, slice_unit=:standard, lmax=obj.lmax, center=[0.,0.,0.], xrange, yrange, zrange, range_unit=:standard, max_bytes=4e9, pos_unit=:standard, verbose=true. A single-cell-thick cut at slice_pos along slice_axis (:x/:y/:z), resampled to a uniform level-lmax buffer (cf. covering_grid for the 3-D version, projection for the integrated map). slice_pos is in slice_unit (:standard ⇒ a fraction of the box). Returns a CoveringGridResult whose grid[var] is a 2-D array.

Off-axis (cutting plane along any line of sight). Triggered by passing any off-axis view keyword — los/inclination/azimuth/axis/theta/phi/direction=:faceon/:edgeon/position_angle/up, or the output controls res/pxsize. The field is sampled on the camera plane through center for an arbitrary orientation (the same view keywords as projection), but as a nearest-cell sample, not an integral — resolution-dependent and not mass-conserving. Returns a NamedTuple with .map, .extent and the camera basis (.los, .up, .cam_right), plus three per-pixel diagnostics: .coverage (which pixels the plane reached), .cellsize (the size of the cell that won each pixel, so comparing it against .pixsize shows whether the map is over-sampled) and .depth (how far along the line of sight that cell sat). There is no cell count: a slice is a nearest-cell painter, exactly one cell owns each pixel, so a count would read 1 everywhere. Empty (NaN) pixels are expected where the plane carries no cell; pass xrange/yrange to fill the frame, or use projection for a conserved map. One variable at a time.

Empty (NaN) pixels are expected in off-axis mode, for two distinct reasons. (1) Without xrange/yrange the frame is the axis-aligned bounding box of the rotated view, and the plane∩box polygon cannot fill that rectangle — the corners and border are NaN. Pass a window inside the box (xrange=…, yrange=…) and the frame fills (0 % empty on a uniform grid). (2) At fine pxsize over coarse AMR cells, nearest-cell sampling leaves sub-percent pixel-scale gaps at refinement boundaries. For a gap-free, mass-conserving map use projection.

offaxis_slice is an alias of this function for the off-axis mode; slice is the name the documentation uses.

sl = slice(gas, :rho, :nH; slice_axis=:z, slice_pos=0.5)          # axis-aligned mid-plane n_H map
sl[:rho]                                                          # 2-D array
oa = slice(gas, :rho, :nH; inclination=60, axis=:angmom,         # off-axis cutting plane
           xrange=[-16,16], yrange=[-16,16], range_unit=:kpc, pxsize=[0.3,:kpc])
oa.map                                                            # 2-D camera-plane array

smart_io_setup(simulation_path::String, output_num::Int; benchmark=false, verbose=true)

Intelligent I/O setup that combines analysis and optional benchmarking.

Returns true when the automatic analysis worked and the settings were applied, false otherwise.

Mera.stellar_age — Method
stellar_age(info::InfoType, birth; unit::Symbol=:Gyr)

Physical age of star particle(s) for a cosmological RAMSES run, from their super-conformal :birth time(s) (scalar or array, as returned by getvar(particles, :birth)). The age is t_proper(a_snap) − t_proper(a_birth) obtained from the Friedmann table (see cosmology); info.time provides the snapshot's conformal time.

unit is a time unit symbol like elsewhere in Mera: :Gyr (default), :Myr, :yr, :s (:standard ⇒ seconds). Non-star sentinels (birth = 0) and any birth time ≥ the snapshot return age 0. This is the conversion used internally by getvar(particles, :age) on cosmological runs.

part = getparticles(info)                 # cosmological run
ages = stellar_age(info, getvar(part, :birth))          # [Gyr]
ages = stellar_age(info, getvar(part, :birth), unit=:Myr)
Mera.storageoverview — Method
storageoverview(dataobject::InfoType; verbose::Bool=true)

Provide a storage overview for loaded data, showing memory usage and data structure information.

Arguments

  • dataobject::InfoType: Simulation info object
  • verbose: Control level of output detail

Description

Displays comprehensive information about the storage characteristics of the selected simulation output. It helps users understand the resource requirements and structure of their data.

For RAMSES outputs it tallies the on-disc size per file type — folder total, and the amr, hydro, gravity, particle, clump, rt and sink files present — returned in a Dict. For other codes (GADGET/AREPO, PLUTO, Athena++, FLASH, Chombo) every quantity is packed into one file or folder, so a per-datatype split is not meaningful; instead the snapshot's disc footprint is reported under :snapshot (the file size for a single-file snapshot, else the folder total).

Examples

# Get storage overview for hydro data
storageoverview(info, true)

# Brief storage information
storageoverview(info, false)
Mera.subregion — Function
subregion(data, shape::Symbol=:cuboid; xrange, yrange, zrange, radius, height, center,
          range_unit=:standard, cell=true, inverse=false, periodic=false, kwargs...)

Select the part of data inside a box, cylinder or sphere given by a shape name.

This older form keeps whole cells and stores no fractions, so totals over the region are not exact at the boundary. Prefer the region form, subregion(data, sphere(...)), which counts boundary cells by the part inside; Mera mentions it once per session.

Arguments

  • data: any Mera data object or a selection.
  • shape::Symbol = :cuboid: :cuboid, :sphere, or :cylinder (also :disc).

Keywords

Shape:

  • xrange, yrange, zrange: [min, max] of a :cuboid, in range_unit and relative to center; missing means the full extent.
  • radius: the radius of a :sphere or :cylinder, in range_unit.
  • height: the half-height of a :cylinder, from -height to +height about center.
  • direction::Symbol = :z: the axis of a :cylinder; only :z is supported, others raise an error.

Position:

  • center = [0, 0, 0]: in range_unit. The default is the box corner, which is right for a :cuboid with absolute ranges; for a sphere or cylinder give [:bc] for the box centre, or a point [x, y, z], or mix them, e.g. [:bc, :bc, 12.0].
  • range_unit::Symbol = :standard: :standard means fractions of the box; any length unit in info.scale works, e.g. :kpc.
  • periodic = false: wrap the region around the faces of a periodic box, true for all axes or per axis as (x=true, y=true, z=false). Without it, the part outside the box is dropped, so a sphere on a face is a hemisphere. Cylinders wrap in their two radial axes only.

Selection:

  • cell::Bool = true: cell data only. true keeps every cell the boundary touches; false keeps a cell when its centre lies inside.
  • inverse::Bool = false: select the outside instead.
  • verbose::Bool = true: print what is selected.
  • myargs::ArgumentsType: set the ranges, radius, height, direction, center, range_unit and verbose in one object. A keyword given directly takes precedence.

Returns

An object of the same type as data, holding only the selected rows.

Examples

julia> s = subregion(gas, :sphere; radius=10, center=[:bc], range_unit=:kpc);

julia> s = subregion(gas, :cylinder; radius=15, height=2, center=[:bc], range_unit=:kpc);

julia> s = subregion(gas, :cuboid; xrange=[-5, 5], yrange=[-5, 5], zrange=[-1, 1],
                     center=[:bc], range_unit=:kpc, cell=false);

See also shellregion, and the region form of subregion above.

Mera.subregion — Method
subregion(data, region; split=true, inverse=false, nsub=8, refine=0, refine_to=nothing,
          verbose=true) -> same type as data

Select the part of data inside a region, counting cells cut by the boundary by the part that lies inside. This is the recommended form.

A region is a value made by sphere, cuboid, cylinder, sphericalshell or cylindricalshell, or a combination of regions with ∩, ∪, \ and !. With split=true each kept cell stores the fraction of its volume inside (:fraction), and getvar(:mass), getvar(:volume) and msum use it, so totals are the amounts inside the boundary and neighbouring regions add up exactly. Works on hydro, gravity, RT, particle, clump and sink data.

Arguments

  • data: the object to select from, or an earlier selection; fractions of repeated cuts multiply.
  • region: the region, e.g. sphere(10; center=[:bc], range_unit=:kpc).

Keywords

  • split::Bool = true: store the fraction inside for cells on the boundary. false keeps a cell whole when its centre lies inside. For particles the default is false: points need no fraction, and split=true is allowed only for gas cells with a :volume (moving-mesh and SPH codes), sized as a sphere of the same volume. Clumps and sinks have no split.
  • inverse::Bool = false: select the outside instead; with split, the fractions are 1 - f.
  • nsub::Int = 8: sample points per axis for cells on a curved or combined boundary. A cuboid along the box axes is computed exactly.
  • refine::Int = 0: cell data only. Subdivide the boundary cells, as an octree, up to this many levels, so the boundary is placed to cellsize/2^refine and maps of the selection have sharper edges. Totals do not change. The cost grows with the boundary area, at most $8^{refine}$ per boundary cell; 2 or 3 is usually enough.
  • refine_to = [length, unit]: subdivide each boundary cell until its children are no larger than this, e.g. the pxsize of a later map. Not together with refine.
  • verbose::Bool = true: print what is selected.

Returns

An object of the same type as data, holding only the selected rows; cell data with split=true also has the :fraction column.

On a 10 kpc sphere the split volume is off by -0.0015 %, the centre test by +0.18 % with no fixed sign, and whole cells by +12 %. For small regions the centre test is off by several per cent; the table is on the Computation Reference page.

Examples

julia> s = subregion(gas, sphere(10; center=[:bc], range_unit=:kpc));

julia> msum(s, :Msol)                                         # the mass inside 10 kpc

julia> disc = cylinder(15, 2; center=[:bc], range_unit=:kpc);

julia> ring = subregion(gas, disc ∩ !sphere(5; center=[:bc], range_unit=:kpc));

julia> stars = subregion(part, sphere(10; center=[:bc], range_unit=:kpc));

See also shellregion, msum, getmask, filterdata.

Mera.subregioncuboid — Method
subregioncuboid(dataobject::GravDataType; kwargs...)

Select a cuboid (rectangular box) subregion from gravity data using AMR-aware filtering.

This function extracts all gravity cells that lie within or intersect a specified rectangular region. It supports both cell-based and point-based selection modes for precise control over boundary handling in adaptive mesh refinement (AMR) simulations.

Arguments

  • dataobject::GravDataType: Input gravity data object from getgravity()

Keywords

  • xrange::Array{<:Any,1}=[missing, missing]: X-coordinate range [min, max]
  • yrange::Array{<:Any,1}=[missing, missing]: Y-coordinate range [min, max]
  • zrange::Array{<:Any,1}=[missing, missing]: Z-coordinate range [min, max]
  • center::CenterType=[0., 0., 0.]: Reference center for ranges
  • range_unit::Symbol=:standard: Units for ranges (:standard, :kpc, :Mpc, etc.)
  • cell::Bool=true: Cell-based (true) vs point-based (false) selection mode
  • inverse::Bool=false: Select outside the region instead of inside
  • periodic=false: Wrap the region around the box faces. true applies to all three axes; a run that wraps in some directions only takes (x=true, y=true, z=false). Needed when the region touches a face: without it the part outside the box is dropped, not wrapped, so a sphere on a face returns a hemisphere. getinfo reports whether the run is periodic (info.boundaries). Cylinders wrap in their two radial axes, never along their height.
  • verbose::Bool=verbose_mode: Print progress information

Selection Modes

  • Cell-based (cell=true): Includes cells that intersect the region boundary
  • Point-based (cell=false): Includes only cells whose centers lie within the region

Returns

  • GravDataType: New gravity data object containing filtered cells

Examples

# Select central 20x20x4 kpc box
subregion = subregioncuboid(gravity, 
    xrange=[-10., 10.], yrange=[-10., 10.], zrange=[-2., 2.],
    center=[:boxcenter], range_unit=:kpc)

# Inverse selection (everything outside the box)
subregion = subregioncuboid(gravity,
    xrange=[0.3, 0.7], yrange=[0.3, 0.7], zrange=[0.4, 0.6],
    inverse=true)

See Also

  • subregioncylinder: Cylindrical subregions
  • subregionsphere: Spherical subregions
  • subregion: Unified interface for all geometries
Mera.subregioncuboid — Method
subregioncuboid(dataobject::RtDataType; kwargs...)

Select a cuboid (rectangular box) subregion from RT data using AMR-aware filtering.

This function extracts all RT cells that lie within or intersect a specified rectangular region. It supports both cell-based and point-based selection modes for precise control over boundary handling in adaptive mesh refinement (AMR) simulations.

Arguments

  • dataobject::RtDataType: Input RT data object from getrt()

Keywords

  • xrange::Array{<:Any,1}=[missing, missing]: X-coordinate range [min, max]
  • yrange::Array{<:Any,1}=[missing, missing]: Y-coordinate range [min, max]
  • zrange::Array{<:Any,1}=[missing, missing]: Z-coordinate range [min, max]
  • center::CenterType=[0., 0., 0.]: Reference center for ranges
  • range_unit::Symbol=:standard: Units for ranges (:standard, :kpc, :Mpc, etc.)
  • cell::Bool=true: Cell-based (true) vs point-based (false) selection mode
  • inverse::Bool=false: Select outside the region instead of inside
  • periodic=false: Wrap the region around the box faces. true applies to all three axes; a run that wraps in some directions only takes (x=true, y=true, z=false). Needed when the region touches a face: without it the part outside the box is dropped, not wrapped, so a sphere on a face returns a hemisphere. getinfo reports whether the run is periodic (info.boundaries). Cylinders wrap in their two radial axes, never along their height.
  • verbose::Bool=verbose_mode: Print progress information

Selection Modes

  • Cell-based (cell=true): Includes cells that intersect the region boundary
  • Point-based (cell=false): Includes only cells whose centers lie within the region

Returns

  • RtDataType: New RT data object containing filtered cells

Examples

# Select central 20x20x4 kpc box
subregion = subregioncuboid(rt, 
    xrange=[-10., 10.], yrange=[-10., 10.], zrange=[-2., 2.],
    center=[:boxcenter], range_unit=:kpc)

# Inverse selection (everything outside the box)
subregion = subregioncuboid(rt,
    xrange=[0.3, 0.7], yrange=[0.3, 0.7], zrange=[0.4, 0.6],
    inverse=true)

See Also

  • subregioncylinder: Cylindrical subregions
  • subregionsphere: Spherical subregions
  • subregion: Unified interface for all geometries
Mera.subregioncylinder — Method
subregioncylinder(dataobject::GravDataType; kwargs...)

Select a cylindrical subregion from gravity data using AMR-aware filtering.

This function extracts all gravity cells that lie within or intersect a specified cylindrical region. The cylinder is defined by a radius, height, center position, and orientation axis. It supports both cell-based and point-based selection modes for precise boundary handling.

Arguments

  • dataobject::GravDataType: Input gravity data object from getgravity()

Keywords

  • radius::Real=0.: Cylinder radius in units specified by range_unit
  • height::Real=0.: Total cylinder height (extends ±height/2 from center plane)
  • center::CenterType=[0., 0., 0.]: Cylinder center position
  • range_unit::Symbol=:standard: Units (:standard, :kpc, :Mpc, etc.)
  • direction::Symbol=:z: Cylinder axis orientation (:x, :y, or :z)
  • cell::Bool=true: Cell-based (true) vs point-based (false) selection mode
  • inverse::Bool=false: Select outside the region instead of inside
  • periodic=false: Wrap the region around the box faces. true applies to all three axes; a run that wraps in some directions only takes (x=true, y=true, z=false). Needed when the region touches a face: without it the part outside the box is dropped, not wrapped, so a sphere on a face returns a hemisphere. getinfo reports whether the run is periodic (info.boundaries). Cylinders wrap in their two radial axes, never along their height.
  • verbose::Bool=verbose_mode: Print progress information

Selection Modes

  • Cell-based (cell=true): Includes cells that intersect the cylinder boundary
  • Point-based (cell=false): Includes only cells whose centers lie within the cylinder

Returns

  • GravDataType: New gravity data object containing filtered cells

Examples

    radius=5., height=4., center=[:boxcenter],
    range_unit=:kpc, direction=:z)

    radius=10., height=0.5, center=[24., 24., 24.],

Select a spherical subregion from gravity data using AMR-aware filtering.

Mera.supports — Method
supports(info::InfoType, what::Symbol) -> Bool

Whether the reader that produced info provides the entry point what (one of :info, :hydro, :particles, :gravity, :rt, :clumps). This is a capability of the READER, not of the snapshot — e.g. supports(info, :hydro) is true for any RAMSES run even if that run wrote no hydro files.

info = getinfo(300, "sim/")        # e.g. a PLUTO run
supports(info, :hydro)             # true
supports(info, :gravity)           # false
Mera.synthetic_clumps — Method
synthetic_clumps(; seed=1, lmax=7, background=:floor, noise=0.0,
                   disk_amp=14.0, disk_hr=0.22, disk_hz=0.10) -> NamedTuple

Build a reproducible, data-free 3-D Mera test bench with a known clump population — no simulation files required. Returns (; gas, particles, truth, info, true_label):

  • gas — a HydroDataType: eight Gaussian density clumps spread through the 2^lmax³ volume (box = 1 kpc), with per-cell velocities and pressure.
  • particles — a PartDataType: one particle bag per clump plus a two-component kinematic stream (for PhaseSpaceFoF).
  • truth — the ground-truth catalog (id, name, kind, pos, mass, width, vsig, bound).
  • true_label — true_label(x,y,z)::Int, the dominant clump id at a position (0 = background).

The data and all finders are fully three-dimensional; the clumps sit at different z, including a touching pair and a kinematically-hot unbound clump. The same eight clumps can be embedded in different environments to test how well a finder separates them from the floor:

  • background=:floor (default) — a flat low background; clumps are isolated islands.
  • background=:galaxy — clumps embedded in a smooth exponential ISM disk (disk_amp, radial/vertical scales disk_hr/disk_hz) whose inner region is itself elevated, so a fixed low threshold captures the diffuse disk as a spurious structure.
  • noise>0 — multiplicative log-normal per-cell fluctuations (turbulent ISM); noise is the dispersion of ln ρ (e.g. 0.2).

Either non-default option fills the whole grid (use lmax=6 to keep it fast). The clump ground truth is unchanged — the background is labelled 0, so a finder that absorbs the floor into its clumps is penalised by clump_recovery.

F = synthetic_clumps()
cat = clumpfind(F.gas, ThresholdFoF(:rho; threshold=5.0, linking_length=2.0/2^7))

See also save_synthetic_clumps, load_synthetic_clumps, and the "Clump Finding — Synthetic Example" documentation page.

Mera.throughput_test — Method
throughput_test(files; runs=1, N=5, levels=[1,2,4,8,16,24,32,48,64])

Reads N files and measures the mean per-file (single-stream) read rate in MB/s per thread count — not summed aggregate bandwidth. Returns (samples, stats, elapsed).

Mera.timed_notify — Method

Track execution time and send notification with timing information

Convenience function that automatically tracks execution time of a code block and sends a notification with timing details.

Parameters:

  • task_name: Description of the task being timed
  • code_block: Function or code to execute and time
  • zulip_channel: Zulip channel for notification (default: "timing")
  • zulip_topic: Zulip topic for notification (default: "Execution Times")
  • include_details: Include detailed performance metrics (default: false)

Examples:

# Time a function execution
timed_notify("Data processing", () -> process_large_dataset())

# Time with detailed metrics
timed_notify("Complex analysis", () -> analyze_galaxy_formation(), 
             include_details=true, zulip_channel="research")

# Time with custom messaging
timed_notify("Simulation run #47", () -> run_simulation(params), 
             zulip_channel="simulations", zulip_topic="Run Times")

max_file_size (25 MB) is the largest attachment sent; larger files are skipped with a warning. Images are resized and compressed toward 1 MB instead.

Mera.timerfile — Method

Get a printout of the timerfile:

timerfile(object::InfoType)

Prints the file and returns nothing.

Mera.timeseries — Method
timeseries(path, reducer; kwargs...)

Run reducer on every snapshot of a simulation and collect the results into a single table — one row per output, ordered by output number.

reducer receives the loaded data object of one snapshot and returns either a scalar or a NamedTuple. The returned table always carries an output column and a time column (physical time in Myr by default, from gettime; a cosmological run additionally gets redshift and aexp columns). A scalar reducer value lands in a value column, a NamedTuple is expanded into one column per field.

Snapshots are loaded strictly one at a time and released before the next, so memory stays bounded — suited to a laptop with limited RAM. Loading respects JULIA_NUM_THREADS (cap it at 4 on a laptop); snapshots are processed sequentially.

Arguments

  • path::String : simulation directory (folder holding output_xxxxx/ for RAMSES, or output_xxxxx.jld2 mera files when mera_files=true).
  • reducer : function data -> scalar | NamedTuple.

Keywords

  • datatype::Symbol = :hydro : :hydro, :gravity, :particles, :clumps, or :rt.
  • outputs = :all : :all, a range (1:10), or an explicit vector of output numbers. Numbers not present on disk are skipped.
  • mera_files::Bool = false : load mera (JLD2) files via loaddata instead of raw RAMSES outputs.
  • loader = nothing : custom info -> data to fully control loading (overrides datatype/ranges/lmax). Use e.g. loader = info -> gethydro(info, [:rho]; lmax=6).
  • lmax = nothing : max AMR level to read (hydro/gravity); nothing uses info.levelmax.
  • xrange, yrange, zrange, center, range_unit : spatial selection passed to the loader — cutting the region is the main lever to reduce RAM per snapshot.
  • time_unit::Symbol = :Myr : unit for the time column — physical by default (:Myr/:Gyr/…); pass :standard for code units (see gettime). A cosmological run also gets redshift and aexp columns automatically.
  • verbose::Bool = true : print per-snapshot progress.
  • notify::Bool = false : call notifyme when finished (a no-op unless ~/email.txt / ~/zulip.txt is configured).

Examples

# evolution of total gas mass and peak density across all outputs
ts = timeseries("/data/sim/timeseries_sedov3d", d -> (
        mass    = msum(d, :Msol),
        rho_max = maximum(getvar(d, :rho)),
     ))

# same, but from mera files and only every region of interest (less RAM)
ts = timeseries("/data/sim/timeseries_sedov3d_mera",
                d -> msum(d, :Msol);
                mera_files=true, xrange=[0.4,0.6], yrange=[0.4,0.6], zrange=[0.4,0.6])

See also checkoutputs, gettime, gethydro, loaddata.

smallr is a density floor passed to gethydro for each snapshot; 0 switches it off.

Mera.update_progress! — Function

Update progress tracker and send notifications if thresholds are met

Parameters:

  • tracker: Progress tracker created by createprogresstracker()
  • current_item: Current item number being processed
  • custom_message: Optional custom message for this update

Examples:

tracker = create_progress_tracker(1000, task_name="Data analysis")

for i in 1:1000
    analyze_data_point(i)
    update_progress!(tracker, i)
    
    # Optional: Add custom message for specific milestones
    if i == 500
        update_progress!(tracker, i, "Reached halfway point - results looking good!")
    end
end

It is called for its effect: it updates tracker and sends a notification when a threshold is passed. Its return value has no meaning.

Mera.update_progress! — Method
update_progress!(tsp::ThreadSafeProgress, filename::String)

Thread-safe function to update progress bar with current file information. Uses locking to prevent race conditions when multiple threads update simultaneously.

Thread Safety

  • Acquires exclusive lock before any modifications
  • Updates both counter and description atomically
  • Releases lock automatically when function exits
  • Prevents progress bar corruption from concurrent updates

Display Format

  • Shows [completed/total] ratio
  • Displays currently processing filename
  • Updates speed calculation automatically
Mera.upgrade_merafile — Method
upgrade_merafile(output, path; datatypes=[:hydro, :gravity, :particles, :rt, :clumps, :sinks],
                 simulation=nothing, parents=true, verbose=true)

Brings a mera file saved by an older Mera up to date: fills grid_info.amr_header from the original simulation (simulation = its directory, default the path recorded in the file) and saves every data type of the file again. With parents=true complete gravity data also gets the stored values of its refined cells (see getgravity(...; parents=true)). Old files load fine without this; the upgrade makes them self-contained, e.g. for writing a RAMSES restart after the simulation is deleted.

Returns the data types that were upgraded, as a Vector{Symbol}.

Mera.usedmemory — Function
usedmemory(object, verbose::Bool=true)
usedmemory(obj_value::Real, verbose::Bool=true)

Calculate and display memory usage of an object or raw byte value in human-readable units.

Arguments

  • object: Any Julia object whose memory usage should be calculated
  • obj_value::Real: Raw memory size in bytes
  • verbose::Bool=true: Whether to print the result to console

Returns

  • value::Float64: Memory usage value in the appropriate unit
  • unit::String: Unit string ("Bytes", "KB", "MB", "GB", or "TB")

Examples

# Check memory usage of a data object
data = rand(1000, 1000)
value, unit = usedmemory(data)  # Prints: "Memory used: 7.629 MB"

# Silent calculation
value, unit = usedmemory(data, false)  # Returns (7.629, "MB") without printing

# Direct byte value
value, unit = usedmemory(1048576, false)  # Returns (1.0, "MB")
Mera.velocitydispersion — Method
velocitydispersion(dataobject; rvar=:r_cylinder, components=(:vr_cylinder,:vϕ_cylinder,:vz),
                   weight=:mass, vunit=:km_s, nbins=50, xrange=nothing, scale=:linear, binsize=nothing,
                   center=[:bc], center_unit=:standard, xunit=:kpc, mask=[false],
                   thermal=false, mu=1.0, Tvar=:T) -> NamedTuple

Radial velocity-dispersion profile. Bins by rvar and returns the per-bin weighted standard deviation of each velocity component — the rest-frame dispersion (about the per-bin mean, so net rotation/streaming does NOT inflate it) — plus the total σ = √(σ₁²+σ₂²+σ₃²) and each component's mean. The default cylindrical triplet gives σR / σφ / σz (use `(:vrsphere,:vθsphere,:vϕsphere)for the spherical decomposition). Each σ is exactly thestdof [profile`](@ref) on that component — this is a convenience wrapper; see the profiles tutorial for the manual recipe.

Returns x (bin centres), edges, count, sigma (total), sigma_components (nbins × n) and mean_components (nbins × n) with the components order, and neff (Kish — small ⇒ noisy σ).

Orbital anisotropy. Pass the spherical triad — exactly (:vr_sphere, :vθ_sphere, :vϕ_sphere), with Greek letters, though the ASCII spellings :vtheta_sphere / :vphi_sphere are accepted — and the result also carries sigma_r, sigma_t = √(σ_θ² + σ_φ²) and the anisotropy parameter beta:

vd = velocitydispersion(part; components=(:vr_sphere, :vθ_sphere, :vϕ_sphere),
                        rvar=:r_sphere, center=cen)
vd.beta            # 1 − σ_t²/(2σ_r²):  0 isotropic, →1 radial, <0 tangential

sigma alone is direction-averaged and hides this. The fields appear only for the spherical triad: β is defined against the spherical radius, and computing it from the cylindrical R would give a different quantity that still looks like a plausible β.

β is blind to sign

It is built from variances, so it says how anisotropic, never which way. Inner-halo radial bias is feedback-driven outflow just as readily as it is infall. Read beta together with the mean radial velocity in mean_components — that is what carries the sign.

β depends on the aperture, and can change sign with it

The dispersion is taken about the mean of whatever region you bin over, so the smoothing scale is part of the measurement. Measured on the same halo, same snapshot:

apertureσ_rσ_tβ (r < 0.5 R200c)β (1–2 R200c)
radial shells (this function)193.8257.9−0.190−0.620
13.7 ckpc/h cells, about each cell's own mean~39+0.438+0.100

Opposite signs, both correct. A shell contains the full spread of large-scale motion — orbiting substructure, rotation — which is tangentially biased; a small cell sees only local turbulence, which is radially biased because it is compressive. Use localdispersion for the small-scale variant.

The actionable form is stronger than "match the aperture before comparing": β has no meaning at all unless the aperture is stated. A paper reporting "β = 0.3 at R200c" is incomplete until it says over what volume the dispersion was taken. State yours.

Dispersions need no rest frame; the means do

Subtracting a constant boost leaves a standard deviation unchanged, so sigma, sigma_components and beta are the same in any frame — no vcenter is needed or accepted here. mean_components is not: for an object with bulk motion those means are box-frame. Get the frame from bulk_velocity and subtract it, or compute the means through getvar with vcenter=.

Thermal & total dispersion (thermal=true). The kinematic σ above is turbulent (bulk) motion only. Set thermal=true to also fold in the gas thermal motion and report the quantity an observer measures as a line width. It adds, per bin:

  • sigma_turb_1d = √(Σσ_i²/n) — the 1-D turbulent dispersion (the sigma field is the 3-D √(Σσ_i²));
  • sigma_thermal = √(k_B⟨T⟩/(μ m_H)) — the 1-D thermal speed at mean molecular weight mu, from the mass-weighted ⟨T⟩. mu=:auto (default) follows getvar(:mu): the per-cell value from the ionization state on an RT run, mass-weighted per bin, and the constant Mera's temperature scaling assumes on a run without RT. Give a number when the thermal speed should be that of an observed tracer rather than of the mean gas (2.33 molecular, 1.0 atomic H, 0.6 ionized);
  • sigma_total = √(sigma_turb_1d² + sigma_thermal²) — the total (turbulent ⊕ thermal) 1-D dispersion;
  • mach = sigma_turb_1d / ⟨c_s⟩ — the turbulent Mach number (⟨c_s⟩ the mass-weighted sound speed);
  • cs, T, mu — the per-bin mass-weighted sound speed / temperature and the mu used.

Tvar selects the temperature field. The default :T_rt is the temperature computed with the local μ, which is the right one in ionized gas and is equal to :T to machine precision on a run without RT, so it is safe either way; pass Tvar=:T for the constant-μ temperature. For a local, patch-de-streamed turbulent ⊕ thermal dispersion (TIGRESS/SILCC-style, removing bulk flow on a chosen length scale rather than per-radial-bin), see localdispersion.

What kind of dispersion this is (3-D, per-annulus)

This is the 3-D, per-bin dispersion: all cells in a radial bin, variance of the velocity component about the single per-bin mean. The bin-mean rotation/streaming is removed, but a velocity gradient across the bin (e.g. the rotation curve varying over the annulus width, a warp, or vertical structure binned only in radius) is not — by the law of total variance σ²_bin = ⟨σ²_local⟩ + Var[⟨v⟩_local], this σ also carries the intra-bin shear term, so it is an upper bound on the local random dispersion. Shrink the bins, or bin in 2-D/3-D (profile3d/phase in R and z or azimuth), to localise it.

For a local, per-pixel dispersion of the line-of-sight velocity instead, use the projected map projection(obj, :σlos) and profile it (profile(proj, :σlos; xvar=:r, weight=:sd)). There σ = √(⟨v²⟩−⟨v⟩²) is taken about each pixel's own mean (the local bulk/rotation velocity is removed per pixel), so it is locally rest-frame; it still includes sub-pixel / along-the-line-of-sight ordered motion (beam smearing). Note these two are also physically different quantities — a 3-D velocity component vs a projected line-of-sight dispersion.

range_unit and center_unit are two names for the unit of center (:standard means fractions of the box); when both are given, center_unit is used.

Mera.verbose — Method
verbose(mode::Union{Bool,Nothing})
verbose()

Set or show the global verbose mode for all subsequent Mera operations. verbose(false) silences Mera's text messages, verbose(true) forces them on (the per-function verbose= argument is then ignored), and verbose(nothing) reverts to each function's own argument (the neutral default). verbose() with no argument prints the current state. See also showprogress and the combined master switch output_mode.

verbose(false)    # quiet
verbose()         # prints "verbose_mode: false"
verbose(nothing)  # back to per-function control

Returns the new setting; without an argument it prints the current one.

viewallfields(dataobject::InfoType)

Mera.viewdata — Method
viewdata(output::Int, path::String="./"; fname="output_", showfull=false, verbose=true) -> Dict

Show what a Mera file written by savedata contains, without loading the data.

For each stored data type it reports the Mera and file versions, the compression, the size on disk and in memory, and the conversion statistics when the file was made by convertdata.

Arguments

  • output::Int: the output number in the file name, e.g. 300 for output_00300.jld2.
  • path::String = "./": the folder of the file; also a keyword.

Keywords

  • fname = "output_": the start of the file name, as given to savedata.
  • showfull::Bool = false: also print the full tree of the file.
  • verbose::Bool = true: print the overview.

Returns

A Dict with one entry per stored data type (e.g. "hydro", "particles") and "FileSize".

Examples

julia> viewdata(300, "mera_files/");

julia> keys(viewdata(300, "mera_files/"; verbose=false))

See also savedata, loaddata, infodata.

Mera.volumelook — Method
volumelook(gas, quantity; unit=:standard, scale=:log, colorrange=nothing,
           percentiles=(1, 99.9), colormap=:inferno, opacity=(0.0, 1.0), opacity_gamma=1.0,
           iso=[], iso_width=0.05, iso_alpha=0.8, exposure=1.0, tone=:aces,
           tau_unit_length=nothing, tau_target=1.5, background=:black, <view keywords>)
    -> VolumeLook

Resolve how a quantity looks: the colour range, the colour map, and how opaque the gas is.

  • colorrange=(lo, hi) in the quantity's unit; without it the given percentiles of the cell values are used. With scale=:log the range is spaced logarithmically.
  • opacity=(a_lo, a_hi) are the ends of the opacity ramp across the colour range, and opacity_gamma bends it (> 1 keeps low values more transparent). Values below the range are invisible when a_lo = 0.
  • iso=[1.0, 10.0, 100.0] adds a surface at each of those values, in the quantity's unit: gas whose value is close to one of them becomes more opaque, so the shell stands out while everything else stays see-through. iso_width is the half width of a shell measured in the units of the colour range (so in dex when scale=:log), and iso_alpha is the opacity added at its centre. Both take either one number for all shells or one per shell; a surface sitting where the data is thin needs a wider band than one where it is dense. A wide outer shell hides the inner ones when they are equally opaque, so give the outer one a smaller value, for example iso_alpha=[0.15, 0.3, 0.6]. With opacity=(0, 0) you see the surfaces alone.
  • exposure brightens the finished picture, like the exposure of a camera. A thin, see-through rendering is dark simply because little light is collected; exposure=3 makes it three times brighter.
  • tone decides what happens to light brighter than white. :aces (default) and :reinhard bend it smoothly, so bright regions fade toward white and keep their detail, the way a camera or a film renderer behaves. :linear cuts it off instead, which holds colours at full saturation and flattens anything bright into one solid patch. Use :linear only when you want the raw numbers on screen.
  • tau_unit_length=[100, :pc] sets what full opacity means: optical depth 1 over that length. Without it the strength is taken from the data, so that the median optical depth of the map is tau_target.
  • background is the colour behind the gas: :black (default), :white, any colour name, or a colour.
  • mask and verbose apply to the automatic strength only: it uses the cells marked true, and verbose=true prints the projection behind it.

The view keywords (direction, inclination, axis, center, range_unit, pxsize, res, xrange…) are only used for that automatic strength, so pass the ones you will render with.

Pass the result to volumerender as look= to keep every frame of a movie identical in colour and opacity.

Mera.volumeorbit — Method
volumeorbit(gas, quantity; angles=0:2:358, sweep=:azimuth, inclination=70, axis=:angmom,
            look=nothing, fov=nothing, fov_unit=:standard, aperture=:circle,
            center=[:bc], parallel_frames=false, <volumerender keywords>)
    -> Vector{VolumeRenderType}

Render a turning sequence with one fixed look and one fixed frame, ready for a movie.

Like rotation_sequence it selects a sphere around center, so every frame shares the same field of view and the object does not appear to zoom. The look is resolved once from the first angle (or taken from look=), so colours and opacity do not flicker.

frames = volumeorbit(gas, :rho; unit=:nH, angles=0:2:358, inclination=70,
                     center=[:bc], fov=20, fov_unit=:kpc, pxsize=[80, :pc])
mkpath("frames")
for (k, f) in enumerate(frames)
    save(joinpath("frames", "f" * lpad(k, 4, '0') * ".png"), rotl90(f.image))
end
moviefromframes("frames", "orbit.gif"; fps=25)

sweep is :azimuth, :inclination or :position_angle. parallel_frames=true renders frames concurrently with one thread each, which fills the cores when there are many frames and uses more memory.

The look keywords unit, scale, colorrange, percentiles, colormap, opacity, opacity_gamma, iso, iso_width, iso_alpha, exposure, tone, tau_unit_length, tau_target and background are those of volumelook, resolved once for all frames. supersample, verbose and max_threads work as in volumerender; other keywords go to the view.

Mera.volumepath — Method
volumepath(gas, quantity; keyframes, frames=120, res=512, fov_unit=:kpc, axis=:angmom,
           look=nothing, aperture=:square, smooth=true, loop=false,
           parallel_frames=false, <volumerender keywords>)
    -> Vector{VolumeRenderType}

Render a camera path: the view moves between keyframes and every frame comes out the same size, ready for a movie.

Each keyframe is a named tuple; anything you leave out keeps its previous value:

fieldmeaning
inclination, azimuth, position_anglethe view angles, in degrees, about axis
fovhalf the width of the picture, in fov_unit; smaller means closer
centerwhere the camera looks, [x, y, z] in fov_unit, or [:bc]

Angles and centres are interpolated in a straight line, the field of view geometrically (so a zoom runs at a constant rate), and smooth=true eases each segment in and out. loop=true returns to the first keyframe at the end, for a movie that repeats without a jump.

frames = volumepath(gas, :rho; unit=:nH, colorrange=(1e-3, 1e2), tau_unit_length=[300, :pc],
    keyframes = [(inclination=0,  azimuth=0,   fov=20),      # face on, wide
                 (inclination=80, azimuth=180, fov=20),      # tilt and turn
                 (inclination=80, azimuth=360, fov=4)],      # turn further and move in
    frames=180, res=512, fov_unit=:kpc)

mkpath("frames")
for (k, f) in enumerate(frames)
    save(joinpath("frames", "f" * lpad(k, 4, '0') * ".png"), rotl90(f.image))
end
moviefromframes("frames", "path.gif"; fps=30)

The camera is orthographic: it looks at the object from outside and cannot fly through it. For a camera inside the gas you need a perspective renderer.

The look keywords unit, scale, colorrange, percentiles, colormap, opacity, opacity_gamma, iso, iso_width, iso_alpha, exposure, tone, tau_unit_length, tau_target and background are those of volumelook, resolved once from the first keyframe. res is the number of pixels across every frame. supersample, verbose and max_threads work as in volumerender, and parallel_frames=true renders frames concurrently with one thread each.

For a plain turn around an object use volumeorbit, which needs no keyframes.

Mera.volumerender — Method
volumerender(gas, quantity; look=nothing, supersample=:auto, <look keywords>, <view keywords>)
    -> VolumeRenderType

Volume rendering of one quantity: every cell gets a colour and an opacity from its value, and the line of sight is composited front to back, so dense gas in front hides what is behind it.

img = volumerender(gas, :rho; unit=:nH, colorrange=(1e-3, 1e2), colormap=:inferno,
                   tau_unit_length=[100, :pc], inclination=70, axis=:angmom,
                   center=[:bc], range_unit=:kpc, pxsize=[50, :pc])
save("galaxy.png", rotl90(img.image))

Opacity is defined per unit length (tau_unit_length), so the picture does not change when you change pxsize or zoom. Without it the strength is taken from the data.

A quantity that fills the whole volume, such as temperature, easily turns into fog: give it a long tau_unit_length (a few hundred pc or more), a narrow colorrange around what you want to see, and opacity_gamma > 1 so that only the top of the range becomes opaque.

iso=[1, 10, 100] puts a surface at each of those values: only gas near them is opaque, so you see nested shells instead of a cloud. With opacity=(0, 0) nothing else is drawn at all.

If the picture comes out dark, the gas is collecting little light along the ray. Any of these helps, in this order: raise exposure; shorten tau_unit_length so the gas is more opaque; narrow the colorrange onto the values you care about; lower opacity_gamma toward 1 so faint gas contributes; or raise the low end of opacity. Choose a colour map that is bright at its low end, such as :viridis or :cividis, rather than one that starts black.

Pass look= from volumelook to keep colours and opacity fixed across the frames of a movie; volumeorbit does that for a turning view.

:vlos, the velocity along the line of sight, works as a quantity even though it is not a getvar field: the renderer knows where the camera is. It is signed, so use scale=:linear, a symmetric colorrange and a diverging colour map such as :vik or :berlin. Positive means moving away from you, as in projection.

View keywords are those of the off-axis projection: direction, los, up, inclination, azimuth, axis, position_angle, angle_unit, xrange/yrange/zrange, center, range_unit, pxsize or res, mask, verbose, max_threads.

The look keywords unit, scale, colorrange, percentiles, colormap, opacity, opacity_gamma, iso, iso_width, iso_alpha, exposure, tone, tau_unit_length, tau_target and background are those of volumelook; with look= they are not used. supersample=:auto renders at up to four times finer pixels where cells are smaller than a pixel and averages them back, within about 1.5 GB of memory; an integer sets the factor.

This is an illustration, not a measurement: the colours and opacity are choices. For physical maps, such as a surface density or an optical depth, use projection. Mera's hints about velocity frames and centres are silenced while a picture is made, since they are about physics rather than about drawing; call getvar yourself to see them.

Mera.withargs — Method
withargs(args; kwargs...) -> ArgumentsType

A copy of args with some fields changed, leaving the original untouched.

Deriving a variant of a bundle otherwise means deepcopy and then assignment, two lines and a mutable original one step away from being edited by accident:

base = ArgumentsType(lmax=10, xrange=[-10., 10.], center=[:bc], range_unit=:kpc)
coarse = withargs(base; lmax=7)          # same region, fewer levels
zoom   = withargs(base; xrange=[-2., 2.], yrange=[-2., 2.])

base is unchanged in both cases.

See also: ArgumentsType, loadall.

Mera.writeramses — Method
writeramses(ncpu; hydro, gravity=nothing, rt=nothing, particles=nothing, sinks=nothing,
            outpath, overwrite=false, verbose=true, force=false, iogroupsize=0,
            ordering=:auto) -> RepartitionReport

Write a restartable RAMSES output with ncpu files from Mera data (loaded with gethydro/getgravity/getrt/getparticles/getsinks, or from mera files with loaddata). The data must be complete (restart_completeness). Refined cells are rebuilt from the leaves, with RAMSES's own averaging rules. Files that leaf data cannot carry (info_rt, stellar_*.csv, …) are copied from the original output if it still exists.

Refused: incomplete data, particle files older than part_file_descriptor.txt (use repartition on the original files), writing into the output the data came from, and a cosmological run from an old mera file whose simulation is gone. force=true writes the last one anyway, and also incomplete data; such an output is for inspection, not for a faithful restart.

iogroupsize = n > 0 writes the files of every n ranks into one group_GGGGG folder, as RAMSES does with IOGROUPSIZEREP = n; RAMSES must then run with the same value. ordering is the domain decomposition, :hilbert or :bisection; :auto takes the run's own (its namelist, or the original output), which the restart namelist names.

Mera.writesidecar — Function
writesidecar(file, source=nothing; note="", anonymize=false, include_script=false, verbose=true)

Write the sidecar <file>.meta.json for a file that Mera did not write itself, typically a figure saved with Makie, PyPlot or Plots. The sidecar is the same as for exportdata: the SHA-256 of the file, the provenance of the snapshot, the input files of the run, the analysis environment and script, and the Julia and Mera that ran.

Call it right after the save:

save("fig3.png", fig)               # Makie
writesidecar("fig3.png", p)         # p: the projection shown in the figure

Arguments

  • file::String: an existing file.
  • source: the Mera result the file shows (a projection, profile, data object, movie or InfoType), or a vector of them for a figure with several panels. With nothing the sidecar still records the file, the analysis and the environment, but no snapshot.

Keywords

  • note::String = "": a free text stored in the sidecar, e.g. "Figure 3 of the paper".
  • anonymize::Bool = false: keep only the last two parts of the simulation path.
  • include_script::Bool = false: also store the full text of the running script (or the code cells of the notebook). Its name and SHA-256 are always recorded, when Julia knows the script.
  • verbose::Bool = true: print the sidecar's path.

Returns

The sidecar's path as a String, or nothing when sidecars are switched off with sidecar(false) or the sidecar could not be written (a warning says why). An existing sidecar of the file is replaced.

Examples

writesidecar("fig3.png", p; note="Figure 3")
writesidecar("evolution.pdf", [p10, p20, p30])   # one panel per snapshot
readsidecar("fig3.png")                          # read it back

See also readsidecar, sidecaroverview, exportdata.

Mera.writesimulationrecord — Method
writesimulationrecord(rec, file) -> String

Write a SimulationRecord to file as readable JSON, and return the file name.

writesimulationrecord(simulationrecord("/data/sim"), "simulation.json")
Mera.wstat — Method
wstat(values::Vector{<:Real}, weight::Vector{<:Real}=[1.0]; mask=[false]) -> WStatType

Return summary statistics of values, optionally weighted.

With weights, the mean, median and standard deviation are weighted; the skewness and kurtosis are computed about the weighted mean without weights. The standard deviation is the population value (divided by N, not N - 1), and the kurtosis is the excess kurtosis, 0 for a Gaussian.

Arguments

  • values: the numbers, e.g. from getvar.
  • weight: one weight per value, e.g. the cell masses. It can also be given as the keyword weight; without it all values count equally.

Keywords

  • mask: a Bool vector with one entry per value; only values marked true count.

Returns

A WStatType with the fields mean, median, std, skewness, kurtosis, min and max.

Examples

julia> s = wstat(getvar(gas, :T, :K), getvar(gas, :mass));   # mass-weighted temperature

julia> s.mean, s.median, s.std

See also average_mweighted, getvar.

Mera.wstat_metaprog — Method

Metaprogramming-optimized statistical functions with template generation. Generates specialized code for different weighting and masking combinations.
